E2E (`--workers=1`): start-moment, compose, mouse-only, edit-latency, lane-rows, lane on chromium-dev and -prod 34/34; mouse-only `--repeat-each=4` 8/8; context-moments, editor-polish, suggestions, open-canvas, palette (dev) 17/17.

## M4.5 mouse-only gaps: undo buttons, chord fill, loop rounding (2026-11-21)
- **Undo and Redo buttons** (`ui/UndoRedo.tsx`): in the transport after Loop, editor layout only. Disabled when their history is empty ("Nothing to undo"); otherwise the tooltip names the entry, "Undo Change chord (⌘Z)". The shortcuts are unchanged. `MOD_KEY` moved from `Menu.tsx` to `platform.ts`.
- **Chord fill** (`editor/new-moment.ts`, `withChordFill`): a new chord without `count`, `byVoice` or an explicit voice target, added where 1 ≤ n lines sound and n < its size, gets `count` = its size (at most the pool). Its size is the pitch list's length or its distinct pitch classes. It applies to palette and shortcut adds and the hover preview (`defaultMoment`), and to suggestions (`suggestionsAt`), so "Open to Am7" on two lines now brings in four. From silence nothing is set, because the establishing rule already gives one voice per note. With n ≥ size, the behaviour is unchanged. No core change: the compiler already births up to `count` from one line.
- **Loop rounding** (`ruler.ts`, `loopTime`): band and edge drags go to the beat grid when `gridBpm` is set (to 1 µs), otherwise to the ms. Alt gives free placement, to the ms. The clamp to the piece end is floored to the ms. The loop stays outside undo.
- **Tests:** unit tests for the fill (editor, suggestions) and `loopTime` (ruler). `mouse-only.spec` now undoes and redoes with the buttons and checks their tooltips and disabled states. It also checks that a dropped chord on New → Empty has count 3, 3 lines and a chord name, not "(unison)"; that loop edges are ms-rounded; and that undo leaves the loop alone.
- **Gates:** lint, typecheck, `pnpm test --maxWorkers=2` 1344/1344. E2E (`--workers=1`): mouse-only, start-moment, palette, loop, suggestions and editor-polish on chromium-dev and -prod, 36/36.

## M4.5-38: compose in play order (2026-11-21)
- **Steps** (`core/generate.ts`): `ComposeStep` gains `phase: 'structure' | 'detail'`; `ComposePass` and the `register`/`fit` passes are gone. Each structural moment is added in its final form (Drift register and short-form scaling applied at placement), strictly left to right. The detail sweep follows, also in time order. `composedPiece` inserts adds in time order, so the file lists moments by time.
- **Detail pass (decision; changes `generate()`).** Generate emitted no refinements, so there was nothing to sweep. Added upper-neighbour notes: on the full split and on each Thin chord, a `note` moment moves the chord's top note up one scale tone and back (`back` 0.9 s, glide 0.6 s, both × the short-form scale), placed once the lines have arrived and ending before the next moment. Over the 200 compose cases there are 445 notes (85 cases have 3, 80 have 2, 30 have 1, 5 have none: the top is already at the range's top). None has a diagnostic, and each produces segments. Only these moments are new: every other moment, id and the end are byte-identical. The two old snapshots are kept and checked against the piece with the `note` moments filtered out. They were not re-recorded, because that check is stronger.
- **Pace** (`app/compose-pace.ts`): a `Beat` has `detail`. The structural pen sweeps once, and detail steps take 700 ms each (≤ 3 s for the sweep), with the pen at the detail's time.
- **App** (`app/compose.ts`): during the structure the camera only eases forward. At the first detail step the whole piece is fitted in view (unless playing), and the camera holds still while each detail is highlighted (`composeView.flash`). This fit is the one deliberate jump: rewinding to the first detail would be the back-and-forth the owner asked to remove. `composeView.pen`/`detail` are exposed as `__algorhythms.compose` for e2e (one line in `debug.ts`). Input changes restart only while building; afterwards they only update the errors. **Compose again** (`composeAgain`, the former "Build again" button) rebuilds, undoing the last build's entry if it is still the newest and untouched, so there is still one entry. The user's own history change closes the panel and the inspector returns. During a build that means a lost gesture; afterwards it means `lastEntry()` differs from the compose's entry, checked through a `useEditor` subscription, while Compose's own history calls run under `ours()`. Edits, undo and redo count; selection and loop changes do not.
- **Tests:** `unit/core/compose-steps.test.ts` covers: replay = `generate()` over 200 cases, with the structure matching the snapshot; the structure monotone and before a monotone detail sweep; moments final when placed; the neighbour notes' shape, timing and sound; prefixes; restart. `unit/app/compose.test.ts` covers: the pen is monotone per phase; the camera moves forward and then holds; no recompose after finishing or Stop; Compose again; a user edit, an undo or an edit mid-build closes the panel. `e2e/compose.spec.ts` covers: a per-frame trace of camera, pen and phase (never back during the structure; held and forward during the details); after-finish inputs wait for Compose again, as one undo entry; undo closes the panel and the inspector returns.
- **Gates:** lint, typecheck; `pnpm test --maxWorkers=2` 1348/1348. E2E (`--workers=1`, chromium-dev and -prod): compose, generate, start-moment 24/24. `test:perf` not run, because there is no compiler or DSP change.
## M4.5-39: lane previews, key at 0:00 (2026-11-21)
- **Lane previews** (`preview.ts`, `Lane.tsx`): a preview (palette hover at the playhead, palette drag over score or lane at the drop time, suggestion hover) now also carries `ghost.lane`, the probe moment's `LaneItem` laid out by `laneItems` from the preview's own compile (span, chord name, establishing point). The lane checks `previewState.ghost` each frame, stacks the ghost with the real items (so it takes, and shows, the sub-row it would get) and places it with the same `place` code; drawn ghosted (dashed marker and bar, italic label, no pointer events; `data-testid="lane-ghost"`, `data-at`, `data-row`). Cleared with the preview: leave, drop, any piece change, and Escape, which now also clears hover previews (a capture-phase keydown while a preview is up; palette-drop still cancels the drop).
- **Key at 0:00** (`editor/piece-key.ts`): `piece.key` stays the source (integrator call, no schema change). The lane shows it as `PIECE_KEY_ID` (`'__pieceKey'`) on a new 18 px **Key row** above Moments (`--key-h`), so the opening chord at 0 keeps Moments sub-row 0. It has no `data-moment`, so existing lane counts are unchanged.
  - **Selection:** `select()` always accepts `PIECE_KEY_ID`. Click or Enter selects it (Shift toggles); Home and ArrowUp from the first item reach it.
  - **Inspector:** shows "Key @ 0:00" with the shared `KeyFields` (now exported, with an optional `write`) → `setPieceKey` (one undo entry), then `offerKeyCarry(PIECE_KEY_ID, old)`. Delete is disabled, with the reason as its tooltip.
  - **Guards:** delete, move and copy act on `piece.moments`, which never holds it, so they are no-ops by construction. A lane drag of it is ignored and doesn't pan. Delete, Backspace and ←/→ on it only announce. With other moments selected, those are deleted or moved alone.
  - **Key carry:** `key-carry.ts` runs the core span helpers on `withPieceKeyMoment(piece)` (the key as a moment at 0, first in compile order; compiles the same) and strips it after Move. No core change.
  - **Adds:** adding a `key` at 0 (palette, K, drop) selects the piece key instead of adding a moment.
- **Rule for an existing user key at exactly 0:00** (`foldOpeningKeys`, applied in `loadPiece` and to the initial piece): unmuted key moments at 0 that run before any other unmuted moment at 0 (array order = compile order there) fold into `piece.key`, the last one winning, and are removed. This is exact: nothing compiled before them heard the old key (unit test compares compiles). A key at 0 after another moment at 0, or a muted one, stays an ordinary moment that overrides from its turn, as before. No example has one.
- **Tests:** `unit/app/piece-key.test.ts` (selection, delete/move/duplicate and the pure recipes as no-ops, group delete/move, add at 0, edit + undo, carry prompt to the first key change + Move in one undo, fold rule and exactness). E2E `lane-preview.spec.ts` (hover ghost at the playhead, marker on the playhead line, own sub-row, Escape; drag over score then lane follows the drop time, Escape; drop clears) and `piece-key.spec.ts` (Key row beside the opening chord, which stays on the first sub-row; select, disabled Delete with tooltip; tonic A→D: degree chord pcs follow; carry prompt; Delete/Backspace do nothing; drag doesn't move; undo).
- **Gates:** lint, typecheck, `pnpm test --maxWorkers=2` 1354/1354. E2E (`--workers=1`) chromium-dev and -prod: piece-key, lane-preview, palette, audition, lane, lane-rows, key-carry, context-moments 60/60; chromium-dev: space-key, suggestions, mouse-only, score-select, editor-polish, inspector, start-moment 31/31.

## Fix: TextField keeps a stale draft over undo (2026-11-21)

`TextField` (chord symbol, label, custom intervals) now uses `draftReduce` like NumberField/WeightBox: typing makes a draft, a changed `value` drops it (render-phase `seen` check), blur/Enter commit only a draft. VoicesDrawer's NumberField already dropped drafts on value change; no other text input had the pattern. Tests: number-draft unit case, chord-symbol case in `number-field-undo.spec.ts`.

## Test hardening against machine load (2026-11-21)
Goal: tests fail only for real bugs, not because the shared Mac is busy (load 10–90).
- **Vitest timeouts** are per file (`vi.setConfig({ testTimeout })`) or per test, about 10× each file's slowest test, measured on 1 worker at load ~15 (the quietest available; JSON reporter). The global 5 s default is unchanged, so light tests still fail fast on a hang.
  - Per-test: unison converge/hold/diverge 52 s → 600 s, held unison 8 s → 90 s; dsp stems "holds through unisons" 36 s → 360 s, "Σ stems = mix" 9 s → 90 s; unit/audio/stems 11 s → 120 s (was 30 s); dsp export unison phrase 4.5 s → 60 s.
  - Per-file: dsp unison 12 s → 120 s, graph 6 s → 60 s, export 4.5 s / stems 5 s → 60 s, arrival, clicks, humanise 1.5 s → 30 s; chord-count-lines 4 s → 60 s; compiler-perf 4 s → 60 s; chord-count 2.6 s, generate-length 2 s, compiler-incremental 1.1 s, lint-rules 1.7 s → 30 s; property suites compiler 1.8 s, schema 1.4 s, distinct-notes, octaves, chord-count 0.8 s, spread → 30 s.
- **No wall-clock budget in unit tests.** compiler.property's 50 ms per-compile check now asserts only with `ALGORHYTHMS_PERF_STRICT=1`; it still logs the slowest compile. Compile speed stays gated by compiler-perf (perf project), and hangs by the watchdog below. No other unit test asserts on `performance.now()`.
- **Property hang watchdog** (`tests/support/fast-check.ts`, `prop-watchdog.ts`): under Vitest, `fast-check` is aliased (vite.config `test.alias`) to the real library with `assert` wrapped. Every property runs with an explicit seed (a drawn one if none is given), and a worker thread counts runs via fast-check's global `beforeEach`/`afterEach`. With no progress for `PROP_HANG_MS` (default 30 s) inside an `fc.assert`, it prints the test name, seed and path to stderr and SIGKILLs the test process, so Vitest reports a failure. A synchronous endless loop can't be caught by fast-check's own `timeout`/`interruptAfterTimeLimit` or Vitest's timeout. Test: `unit/prop-watchdog.test.ts`.
- **E2E: wait for the condition, don't sample a moment.**
  - Sound waits are 20 s (app, audition) and 30 s (audio fixture A4); those tests are `test.slow()`.
  - Playback-rate checks measure over 3 s (app) and 2 s (fixture clock) instead of 1.5 s and 0.6 s.
  - view-follow: Smooth polls until a 600 ms run of frames holds the playhead within 1 % of 35 %; Page samples window by window until it turns, with the same assertions; Off polls until the playhead advances, and asserts the view never moved.
  - compose: polls the moment count instead of fixed waits. mouse-only: polls until the time moves.
- **`@perf` tag** for timing gates: edit-latency, app-perf (60 fps), render budget, time to first sound, mouse-only (it ends with the latency budget), stems memory. `pnpm e2e:quick` runs `--grep-invert @perf` for local runs on a loaded box. CI is unchanged and runs everything.
- **Gates:** with 3 CPU burners (load 35–52): `pnpm test -- --maxWorkers=3` twice, 1361/1361 each; e2e app, audio, audition, view-follow and compose on chromium-prod and firefox-dev (`--workers=2`, `--grep-invert @perf`) three times, 73 passed / 7 skipped each. Without burners: lint, typecheck, `pnpm test --maxWorkers=2` 1361/1361; mouse-only on chromium-dev passes.
## R9-1: parts (core, 2026-11-21)
PLAN rev 9 "Parts": `VoiceDef.part?: string` and the `{ part }` target, in schema v2 with no version bump (M4.5-27: the 1 → 2 migration needs nothing for an optional field). `core/parts.ts` (`partOf`: a missing or non-string part is the default group `''`; `selectorPart`); `VoiceRun.part`; `MomentEnv.part(v)` (the part of a logical voice's line).

**Merges stay within a part** (every 29a join path):
- coincidence joins (`onJoin`) only group lines of one part; establishing voices stack only within a part;
- explicit merge and chord `count` deaths: `absorbers` takes the nearest order neighbour of the dying line's part; with none, it fades where it is (as a merge with no survivors);
- newborns: `Compiler.partBirths` gives each birth a free voice of its parent line's part; with none it is dropped (`pool-exhausted`, warn). Phantoms (`expand`) and fork carriers (`collapse`) are voices of the line's part; `collapse`'s held notes are keyed per part, so a newborn bound for another part's note is born and does not join it;
- free octaves: `distinctTargets` takes a separate `occupied` list: the untargeted lines of the targeted lines' parts (`split.ts occupying`, also the `sounding` of split/count-birth voicings). The room beyond the register (`octaveRoom`) is still bounded by every untargeted line, so free octaves don't step past another part's line.

**Decisions (PLAN is silent):**
- Moments spanning parts (`all`, `rank`, `voices`) are voiced across all their lines as before (one chord over all, its targets still distinct among themselves); only the merge rules above are per part.
- A `{ part }` moment sees only its part: `env.active` and `env.dormant` are the part's, so `count` on split, merge and chord counts the part's lines, establishing from silence uses the part's voices, and no-effect reasons and the palette (`effect.ts`) read the part.
- An unknown part: `unknown-part` (warn, registered), targets nothing.
- `crossing-untargeted` reports only lines of the moved lines' parts (parts cross freely, like a tune).
- Incremental: a change of a voice's part forces a full compile (`sameSetup`).

**Not in scope (needs the integrator):** `src/app/editor/walk-steps.ts` `targets()` must handle `{ part }` or typecheck fails (`sel.rank` on the new union member). The change, refused to this builder as outside `src/core`/`tests`:
`if ('part' in sel) return partOf(piece.voices[v]) === sel.part;` after the `'voices'` line, importing `partOf` from `core/parts`. `docs/file-format.md` regenerated (`pnpm docs:schema`).

**Goldens:** unchanged (no example has parts; without parts every path is the old one).
**Tests:** `tests/unit/core/parts.test.ts` (pin onto another part's pitch, unison across parts, merge/count death into own part, merge with no own-part survivor, newborn part, free octaves ignore another part vs. without parts, `{ part }` target, part counts, unknown part, establish, palette, schema round trip, incremental). Generators: `pieceArb({ parts: true })` (voices in 'A', 'B' or none; `{ part }` targets incl. unknown 'C'). Properties: distinct notes per part, no death/birth across parts, joins within a part (red, with the unit tests, when `onJoin` ignores parts), incremental = full with parts.
**Merged main** (0a473e7: M4.5-38/39). The only conflict was the build log; both sides are kept.
**Sweeps:** `PROP_RUNS=1000` over `tests/property/core` with the budget lifted locally only. First set: 3 of 4 runs completed, and the fourth hung in a sync loop (seed unknown, since random seeds aren't printed; not reproduced). Second set, with logged seeds 485958476, 460949692, −320129613, −75233451: 3 green. The failing one (chord-count "spaced pieces" −320129613/300) fails identically on main's core, and so do two earlier crossings seeds (−883975252/819, −440599787/662). None comes from parts; all are left open.
**Gates:** lint and `pnpm test --maxWorkers=2` (1377/1377) pass, and so does `pnpm test:perf` (5/5, load ~9). Typecheck fails only on `walk-steps.ts`, as above.

**R9-1 app fix:** `walk-steps.ts` `targets()` handles `{ part }` (voices whose `partOf` equals it); test `walkAt with a { part } target` in `tests/unit/app/walk-steps.test.ts`.
Inspector `targetMode` has no part mode yet (R9-6): a `{ part }` target falls through to the `rank` mode with no range shown. Typecheck is clean.

## No-GC lock flake (2026-11-21)
CI failed `no-gc` 'lock' twice with "0 GC lines, 1 observed". **Cause (a), with a (b) symptom.**
- **Real allocation:** the measured minute allocated 102,792 B of young generation in every run (plain: ~1.9 KB, the harness's own timer and flag calls). A heap sampling profile put all of it in code that runs only on a seek or a birth: `probe` ~70 KB, `applyTransport` → `jump`/`isSounding`/`resetNorm`/`ghostOf` ~24 KB, `publishPhase` 5 KB. `--trace-opt`: none of them was ever optimised (12 seeks a minute), and the interpreter boxes every double. The hot paths allocated nothing.
- **Why "1 observed, 0 traced":** reproduced locally in 1 of 30 runs: a minor GC starting 0.1 ms after the run, outside the `--trace-gc` window, set off by the first allocation after the run in a young generation the run had filled ~100 KB further. The observer counted the 50 ms waits around the run too.
- **Second cause, found after warming those paths (200+ seeks):** 15 KB a minute stayed, all in the optimised `probe`. TurboFan's 920-byte inlining budget went on lastAtOrBefore ×3 (219), warpOffset ×2 (194), evalFlatSegment (157) and lifeWeight (172), so segmentPitch (491 with easeRaw) was called and boxed its 13 arguments on each probe.
- **Fixes:**
  - `VoiceData.probe` writes out the life weight, evalFlatSegment and warpOffset (now 710 B inlined, segmentPitch included). It is bit-identical: tests/dsp/probe.test.ts compares it with the old functions. `lifeWeight` and `smootherstep` are gone; `warpOffset` stays as the reference.
  - The harness's warm-up seeks 1000 times (every 4 blocks) before its seek cycle, so the seek and birth paths are optimised.
  - The observer counts only GCs that start inside the measured span.
  - The test also gates the young generation's growth over the run, `newSpaceBytes` ≤ 6000 B in plain and lock. So any allocation fails on every run, not only when it happens to tip a GC. Now 1864 B in both modes. It would have been 102,792 B before the fixes, and ~12.8 KB with the warm-up but the old probe.
- **Left (V8 tiering, not fixable in `process()`):** after a page load, the seek and birth paths run interpreted for their first ~200 seeks. In this 12-voice stress that is ~100 KB a minute, so a short scavenge every few minutes of constant seeking.
- **Stress:** 60 'lock' runs, two at a time beside two CPU burners (load ~12), half with `--max-semi-space-size=1`: 0 GC lines, 0 observed, 1864–1872 B in all 60.
## R9-6: parts UI (2026-11-21)
`src/app/editor/parts.ts` (pure recipes: `partNames`, `assignPart`, `renamePart`, `deletePart`, `patchPart`, `setPartRangeBound`, `common`); each drawer action is one `edit` (one undo entry, the usual incremental/hot-swap path; a part change forces a full compile per R9-1).
- **Drawer:** without parts it is as before plus a **New part** header button. With parts, one `<tbody>` per part (pool order of first appearance), then **Unassigned** (a plain header, no controls). A part header row holds a name field (Enter/blur commits; empty or taken names refused, error in the tooltip), × delete, and timbre, pan (A + slider), gain and range for all its notes, plus "n notes". A Part column gives each note a select: Unassigned, the parts, **New part…**.
- **Inspector:** `PartField` (Part select: All, the parts) after Time whenever the piece has parts (or the moment targets a part). `targetMode` gains `'part'`; a part target keeps Advanced closed with no chip pressed. Header shows "· Part"; the lane tooltip reads "Part: X" above any no-effect reason.

**Decisions (PLAN is silent):**
- New part (header): no parts → every note goes into "Part 1"; with parts → the unassigned notes form "Part n" (disabled when none). Row "New part…" makes "Part n" holding that note.
- "Mixed": timbre select shows a disabled "Mixed" option (as All notes); pan/gain readouts say "Mixed" while the slider sits at the first note's value and a drag sets all; range fields are blank with a "Mixed" placeholder, and committing one bound sets that bound on every note, keeping each note's other bound. Pan "A" sets all auto, or all to centre when all are auto.
- Rename to an existing name is refused (no merging parts).
- Delete part: notes become unassigned; moments targeting it are retargeted to `{ voices: [its notes] }` (default target if it had none); asks inline in the drawer when any moment targets it.
- Part chooser: a rank/pick target shows a disabled "Advanced" option; an unknown part shows as "X (no notes)".
- Pool growth doesn't copy a part; new notes are unassigned.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/app/parts.test.ts` (rename + undo, refused names, whole-part set, range bound, move, delete, target-mode round trip); `tests/e2e/parts.spec.ts` (build parts, Part chooser, header/tooltip, diagnostics reach the UI, rename as one undo, whole-part timbre + Mixed, All, delete confirm).

## R9-3a: the `pulse` moment (core, 2026-11-21)
PLAN rev 9 `pulse` in core: types (`PulseMoment`, `PulseRecord`, `CompiledVoice.pulse`), schema (defaults step 0.5, pattern [1], decay 0.3, floor 0, mode together; `strike` optional = true), compile, incremental, flat layout, reference envelope.
- **Records** (per voice, sorted, pushed at the simulation time): `{ t, accent, decay, floor, strike, hit? }`. A hit is one step; a boundary (accent 1, decay 0) puts a voice at `floor` when it joins a pulse, or back at 1 (floor 1) at `until`.
- **Envelope** (`segment.ts pulseEnvelope`, leaf, + − × only, so the DSP can call it as is; `pulse.ts pulseGainAt`): the last record with t ≤ now applies: accent·(floor + (1 − floor)·(1 − u)³), u = dt/decay; accent·floor once u ≥ 1. 1 before the first record. Multiplies level and life fades.
- **Steps** are tick events (`at + k·step` while < until, then one at `until` that ends it); a pulse tick changes no pitch, so no cache invalidation. Its walk state rides on the queued event (immutable), so checkpoints need only the per-voice pulse length and owner (NUMS 18 → 20).
- **Flat layout v4:** `FlatVoice.pulses`, PULSE_STRIDE 6 (T, ACCENT, DECAY, FLOOR, STRIKE, HIT), constants in segment.ts, re-exported by flat.ts. Transfer list gains one buffer per voice.

**Decisions (PLAN is silent):**
- Targets resolve at each step as for any moment (active voices; `{ part }` works). Only sounding voices are hit; dying voices (merge) are no longer targetable, so they leave the walk and keep their last envelope while they fade.
- A rest (accent 0) hits nothing and does not advance the walk. Accents and floor are clamped to 0..1.
- Arpeggio order: the sounding targeted voices by their pitch at the step instant (ties: pool index). up/down/updown keep their index when voices join or leave (clamped), updown bounces without repeating ends (0 1 2 1 0). random: `randHashed(momentSeed, hash(''), k, PulsePick)` (new Purpose 16), uniform, repeats allowed.
- A voice born inside a pulse that targets it sits at the floor from its birth (boundary at the birth), not from the next step.
- Overlaps: the later-started pulse (later `at`, then array order) drives a voice; it claims at its moment event, so an earlier pulse's step at the same time skips it. When it ends, the voice goes to the latest earlier pulse still running that targets it (at that pulse's floor), else back to 1.
- A pulse's region counts as activity (end = last activity + 4 s), like a walk.
- `no-effect` when no targeted voice sounded at any step, raised at `until` (none if the piece ends first). `pulse-invalid` (error): step ≤ 0, until ≤ at, empty or non-numeric pattern. Schema rejects an empty pattern, step ≤ 0 and accents outside 0..1; until ≤ at is left to the compiler, as for walks.
- The palette list (`EFFECT_MOMENT_TYPES`) leaves `pulse` out until the editor makes one (the app's `defaultMoment` has no case); `momentEffectAt` handles it.

**Needs the integrator (outside src/core):**
- Typecheck fails on three app records/switches: `actions.ts` LABELS and `Inspector.tsx` TYPE_NAMES need `pulse: 'pulse'` / `pulse: 'Pulse'`; `new-moment.ts` needs a `case 'pulse'`, e.g. `m = { ...base, type: 'pulse', until: at + 4, step: 0.5, pattern: [1], decay: 0.3, floor: 0, mode: 'together' }`.
- Render (R9-5 hook, `src/render/level.ts`): `pulseLevel(v, t)` → `pulseGainAt(v, t)` from core/pulse, and `opacityBreaks` adds `pulseBreaks(v)` (records and uncut decay ends; not flat when `v.pulse` is non-empty; cache on `voice.pulse` too). Not done here: another builder is in src/render.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/core/pulse.test.ts` (envelope, walk order, together/strike/floor/decay/cycling, up/down/updown/random, crossing glide, births and deaths, `{ part }`, overlaps, diagnostics, schema, palette, incremental = full over edits inside the region, flat round trip); pulse in `pieceArb`; property "pulse records" in compiler.property. Goldens unchanged.
**Gates:** lint clean; typecheck clean in core/tests (the three app errors above); `pnpm test` 1416/1416 after the schema message fix; `test:perf` 5/5 (load ~21). 1000-run sweeps (seeds logged): incremental 7197195 and chord-count 784328314 green; compiler 893200642 fails "no crossings" and distinct-notes 1028843820 fails "no two resting lines share a pitch". Both counterexamples compile to identical segments and lives with their pulses removed, so neither comes from pulse; left open with the earlier crossings seeds.

Not caused by this merge; needs a look at `core/ease`.
## Dense lanes: capped lane area + compact rows (owner bug, 2026-11-21)
Kite Day at 1024×768 pushed Transitions and Level off-screen (lane 638 px; the stage's 240 px min-height plus "the lane grows to fit"). Integrator's design (owner may veto):
- **Cap:** `laneMaxPx` (editor/lane.ts) caps the lane slot at 45 % of `.timeline` minus ruler and gap (floor 60 px), set by Lane.tsx on resize; the stage's min-height is 0 in the editor layout. Rows beyond the cap scroll inside `.lane-scroll`; a fade marks rows hidden above/below. Time axis, pan and zoom unchanged.
- **Wheel:** `wheel.ts` `wheelIntent` shared by score and lane: Ctrl/Cmd/Alt zooms, a horizontal trackpad or **Shift-wheel pans** (Shift is new on the score too), a plain vertical wheel scrolls the lane area natively.
- **Compact rows:** `laneStack`: when the labelled packing needs > 3 sub-rows, repack without labels (`compactBoxPx`: marker + span) on 8 px Moments / 7 px Transitions sub-rows (`[data-compact]`). Labels float on hover, focus or selection and lead the tooltip; bars lose their length text; bar hit target +1 px each side. ≤ 3 sub-rows: unchanged. Deviation: 7 px transition sub-rows (asked ~8–10) to cut scrolling.
- **Reveal:** a newly selected moment (score, inspector, keys, hook) scrolls its marker into view (`revealScroll`); keyboard focus too.
- **Sizes:** Kite Day 1024×768: timeline 341, score 188 (55 %), lane area 113 of 180 px (8 compact sub-rows; 67 px scroll to reach Level). 1280×720: score 230/417, lane 147 of 180. Tidewater Hymn: no compaction, no scroll. Kite Day can't fit unscrolled at 1024×768: the spans alone need 8 sub-rows (markers alone 6).
- Clockwork Heron at Fit also goes compact (piece-key.spec now compares marker centres).
- Tests: tests/unit/app/lane.test.ts (laneStack, rowGeometry, laneMaxPx, revealScroll, wheelIntent); tests/e2e/lane-dense.spec.ts.
## R9-2: land on the beat, staggered splits and merges (core, 2026-11-21)
PLAN rev 9 "Land on the beat" (§5 `Glide.land`, §11.3) and "Split and merge honour stagger and order" (§18).

**`glide.land?: boolean`** (types, `GlideSchema`/`PartialGlideSchema` optional with no default, so files round-trip unchanged; `DEFAULT_GLIDE` has none: absent = false; `sanitizeGlide` keeps a boolean). `compiler/glide.ts`: `landing(at, glide)` (start = at − length − stagger; before 0 it clamps to 0 and scales length and stagger by at/(length + stagger)), `decisionTime(m, defaults)` / `pieceDecisionTime(piece, m)`, `glides(m)` (land applies to point moments, not key, level or walk).
- The moment event is queued at the lead-in start (`Compiler.eventT`); everything it decides (target, cancellation, voicing, planning, no-effect, harmony entry) happens there. Under 'together' the planner ends every move at `at` (`PlanHost.land`); under 'spread' timings run from the start as usual.
- **For R9-9:** `momentSpans` already give the bar: start = earliest move start, end = `at` (together). A land moment with no moves has its span at `at`. `landing()` gives the nominal window without a compile.

**Decisions (PLAN silent):**
- A lone voice under 'together' glides length + stagger (the window is fixed before the voice count is known). Under 'spread' the last stratum lands within stagger/n of `at`, ± jitter.
- A moment whose marker falls inside a land moment's lead-in is decided later and interrupts it (§7.1 event order); no special precedence.
- A land moment reads the key in effect at its `at` (key moments at the same time count in array order, `keyFor`), so a degrees chord landing on a key change uses the new key.
- A land moment whose lead-in finds nothing sounding is re-queued at `at` (`SUB_LANDED`) and acts there without a lead-in, so an establishing chord comes in on the beat.
- Incremental: `firstChange` uses decision times, and a changed key moment also pulls in land moments that decide before it and read it. Land re-queues are saved in checkpoints like note returns; `restore` compares spans by `eventT`.
- State reads at the decision time: `effect.ts` (`readState`'s "decided by t", `momentEffectAt`, `momentEffectsAt`, with the marker's key), `freezeChord` (moments decided before it, plus keys before its `at`), `transpose.ts` note matching. `suggestNext` still analyses the harmony at the cursor; each suggestion's effect check reads its decision time.

**Fix (latent, exposed by land):** `dormantAt` no longer offers a dying voice whose close falls at the moment's time (the close runs after moment events): an establishing moment reused it, the close then ended its new life, and the voice stayed Active with no pitch, hanging `assignInjective` on a NaN (sweep seed 731906245; regression `land-close-seed731906245.json`). Land arrivals fall exactly on marker times, so deaths now end exactly where later moments start.

**Splits and merges:** the planner now draws stratified, `order`-ranked offsets over every mover, newborn and dying voice (rank key: start pitch, newborns their parent's; then target; then pool index). Newborns no longer start at their parent's departure or a `Purpose.Birth` draw (now unused); a newborn forks from its parent's pitch at its own start (mid-glide if the parent is moving). A dying voice no longer adopts its moving survivor's timing up front; the crossing rule adopts when they would cross. Siblings of one parent forking outward in order would start out of order with each other, so the crossing rule times them together (expected).

**Goldens** (owner-approved sound change): the five originals changed timing only; compositions unchanged (no splits or merges). Duration, segment and life counts, last pitches, diagnostics and span counts are identical. Retimed vs. main: clockwork-heron 19 births / 68 segments (max 0.68 s); lanterns 20 / 108 (0.42 s); seven-doors 22 / 61 (0.43 s); cartographers-rain 25 / 93 (1.20 s); tidewater 22 / 67 (0.33 s). Re-pinned: `examples.golden.json`, `arrive.test.ts` PRE_REV6, `migration-v2.test.ts` V1_AUDIBLE (originals).

**Tests:** `tests/unit/core/land.test.ts` (together, spread, lone voice, clamp, `decisionTime`, overlap both ways, key at the marker, establishing from silence, effect check vs compiler, freezeChord, incremental with lead-in/land/key edits, schema; split and merge ranks for each order; land on births and deaths). Adjusted for the new timing: compiler-moments (cancelled newborn: 3 newborns, high-first), chord-count-lines (fork pitch = parent's at birth; held parents still held), property-seeds-29 (shift at the pin's time; sign-change crossing check; /429 no longer sets up its waiting fork, kept as a no-artifact regression). Arbitraries: `land` in default and moment glides.
**Sweeps:** `PROP_RUNS=1000`, budget lifted locally only: compiler 731906245, distinct-notes −1290388471, incremental 48151623, chord-count 1999000331, all green (after the `dormantAt` fix; the first compiler run hung on it).
**Gates:** lint, typecheck, `pnpm test:perf` 5/5. `pnpm test --maxWorkers=2` at load 30+: 20 failures, all timeouts; rerun serially, all pass but two dsp `unison.test.ts` timeouts (5 s, load ~20; they passed serially earlier).

Merged main; wired `pulse` into LABELS, TYPE_NAMES, `defaultMoment` (not in the palette), and render `pulseLevel`/`opacityBreaks` (core `pulseGainAt`/`pulseBreaks`; cache also keyed on `voice.pulse`). Tests: render level (pulse hit and between hits), e2e inspector "Pulse". Regenerated docs/file-format.md. Full `pnpm test`: only the property failures remain (distinct-notes known; "walk steps never overwrite a point moment" seen once, unchecked against main).

## Thickness transitions: strands and ropes (render, 2026-11-21)
Owner (via integrator): no taper after the meeting; meeting lines bundle like strands into a rope, and a split peels strands off it. Drawing only: compiled weights, lives and sound unchanged.
- **Bundles** (`render/strands.ts`, cached per score): a merge is a death whose hand-over time (died − fadeOut) matches a weight rise of a line on the same pitch (the survivor); a split is a birth matching a parent's weight fall on the same pitch. Members are ordered by pitch (highest on top). Merged lives are drawn ending at the meeting (no fade), split strands start unfaded.
- **Geometry:** over the window, each strand's width goes from √w·base to its share (w/W of the rope's √W·base), and its centre moves to its slot, so at the meeting the strands tile the rope exactly. The merged line starts there at full width. Splits mirror this: the strands tile the parent's drawn width just before the birth, hold until they depart, then peel. One smootherstep mix per strand (`strandMix`, 0 = on its own, 1 = bundled) drives width and offset; a later core loudness hand-over can follow the same curve. Window: max(32 px, ⅓ of the converging or departing glide), clipped at the member's neighbouring bundles. Each strand overlaps the one below by ≤ 0.4 base widths (at most that strand's width), so anti-aliased seams don't leave the tiled rope lighter than the line it becomes. Weight changes that aren't merges, splits or births (edits) taper with a smootherstep over max(their ramp, 32 px).
- **Drawing** (`draw.ts traceWeighted` → `outline.ts`): lines whose width, place and opacity never change are still one stroke. Any other line is filled as a variable-width outline: centre ± w/2 along a mitred normal, sampled ≤ 2 px wherever geometry or opacity varies. There is one shape per run of equal opacity and thin slices (Δα ≤ 0.025) through opacity ramps. Ends are straight wherever pieces meet; round ends appear only at true line ends and at the call's ends. Highlights, the dotted trace, heads (offset and drawn opacity) and the player use the same geometry.
- **level.ts:** `opacityIn`/`breaksIn` take the drawing's lives; `opacityAt`/`opacityBreaks` unchanged.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/render/strands.test.ts` covers detection, tiling (shares sum to √W, edges meet), continuity, mix, the edit taper, the outline maths (widths, caps only where asked, shared seams, bends) and drawScore (no round ends at seams). `weight.test.ts` and `level.test.ts` now read fills. `tests/e2e/thickness.spec.ts` uses an 8-line unison and split: it reads the canvas back, narrows steadily into the meeting with no neck, keeps a constant rope width, then widens out of the split. It fails on the old drawing.
- **Perf** (`render.spec` @perf, chromium-dev, load ~20): before: ink-24 flush 3.5 ms, pitch draw-only 1.0, overview 5.6. After: 2.9, 1.3 and 3.7.
- **Status:** lint, typecheck green; `pnpm test --maxWorkers=2` 1410/1410; e2e:quick thickness, render, score-select, silent-lines and dots on chromium-dev and firefox-dev: 17 passed, 15 skipped (chromium-only specs).
- **Notes:** Kite Day has no merges or splits (compiled), so its 0–20 s view is unchanged; Tidewater 82–92 s (12-line unison, staggered burst) shows it. A dying line's head is hidden after the meeting, so the Hz label no longer says "×12" on a unison.
## Fix: smootherstep monotone near 1 (2026-11-21)
- The flaky "standard eases … monotone" property was smootherstep, not a 3e-6 dip: fast-check printed its *inputs* `[code 0, x, x]`. Near x = 1 the direct polynomial cancels (`x(6x − 15) + 10 → 1`) and dipped by up to 4e-15, beyond the old 1e-15 slack.
- Fix: for x > 1/2 evaluate `1 − s(1 − x)` (point symmetry; `1 − x` is exact). Output changes ≤ 2.1e-15; no other ease changes. No-GC gate still passes.
- The-cartographers-rain's compile moves by 7e-15 st in 2 numbers (structure unchanged), so its pinned hashes in examples.golden.json, migration-v2 and arrive tests are updated (outside the ease scope, but forced).
- The property's slack is now 4·ε (cosine and smoothstep still round by 1 ulp of 1); with the old code it fails 8 of 10 times at 20 000 runs, with the fix 0 of 30. Regression unit test at the seed-353562081 inputs plus a dense sweep below 1.
## Dense lanes v2: rows by kind + divider (owner rejected compact rows, 2026-11-21)
Replaces 54f39c1's compaction (PLAN §11.3 "Dense lanes"). Kept: the cap, internal scroll, fades, wheel mapping, scroll-into-view.
- **Rows:** `laneRowOf` (editor/lane.ts): `level` → Level row, `pin` → new Pins row (shown only when pins exist, incl. a pin ghost), the rest → Moments/Transitions at full height. `laneLayout` stacks each row: Moments by label+span (as before), Pins by span overlap (12 px sub-rows, marker and glide bar on one line), Level by marker collision (sub-row 0 sits on the envelope via `curveAt`; colliders go to 14 px strips below). Level items have no bar (ramp = envelope), so a level's `over` is edited in the inspector only. Pins/Level labels float on hover, focus or selection; tooltips carry the name.
- **Divider** (`.lane-divider`, the old 10 px gap): drag, ↑/↓ (Shift/Page: ×4), Home/End, Enter or double-click resets. Clamps (`clampLanePx`): score ≥ 25 % of the timeline, lanes ≥ 44 px and ≤ content. Stored as the lane share of the timeline in localStorage `algorhythms.laneSplit` (lane-split.ts, try/catch); default stays `laneMaxPx` (55 %).
- **Kite Day 1024×768:** timeline 341, score 188, lane area 113 of 232 px content (Moments 64 = 3 sub-rows at 20 s zoom, Transitions 46, Pins 28, Level 74 = 4 sub-rows: several levels share times). 1440×900: lane 198, no scroll.
- Level sub-rows are common, not rare: Kite Day levels often share a time across voice groups (4 sub-rows even zoomed in).
- Tests: lane.test.ts (row assignment, `laneLayout`, `curveAt`, split clamps, storage); lane-dense.spec rewritten; lane-rows (level on Level row, stacking test uses a shift); lane-preview (level/pin ghosts' rows; a racy ghost-time check now polls).
## Moment-end pop (dsp, 2026-11-21)
- **Owner report (version 29):** "a light pop at the end of every moment".
- **Bisect:** Kite Day's offline render (`renderOffline`, 48 kHz, 20 s) is bit-identical at 9bd2e44 (version 25) and 5d1f216. No audio change in that range; R9-2 moves Tidewater's splits from 9.5 s on (by design). Live worklet output (Chromium, Kite Day) matches offline on every detector, with no extra engine messages during playback.
- **What happens at a moment end:** per-voice amplitude traces (one value per block) and per-voice stems were checked at every glide arrival, level-ramp end, weight-ramp end, birth and death. Pitch is C¹ at every end. The amplitude has one corner: the **articulation re-strike** (M4.5-32). Glass rises from 0.65 to 1.0 (+3.7 dB) in 12 ms on every arrival, and under `arrive: 'together'` every chord voice strikes on the same sample. Organ (the bass pins) strikes +14 %, and the walk's glass voices strike every 0.3 s. With articulation off, the worst second difference at ends drops from 0.126 to 0.003 of the level. There is no sample discontinuity, no HF burst (≤ 1.4× steady, 3 kHz residual), no LF thump (−48 dB) and no limiter action. So the pop is the strike, as designed. Whether it should be softer (less amount, a longer attack, or no strike on merges: "no swell after arrival") is a timbre call for the owner. Nothing changed.
- **Found on the way (older than 9bd2e44, not at an end):** when gliding lines meet during a merge, the unison group gain h (`computeGroups`, 2 ms `LOCK_GAIN_SMOOTHING`) steps by about 30 % in one block (v6 1.0 → 1.31, then 1.41, then back within 20 ms). At 9bd2e44 it was a 19 % dip at the same time. Not fixed: the fast smoothing is how h follows capture beats. Left for the merge crossfade work (PLAN: merges bundle and crossfade across the approach).
- **Tests** (`tests/dsp/clicks.test.ts`): a compiled piece with a chord arrival, a pin, level ramps, a split and a merge.
  - Sample test (sine, glass): the click score at every kind of end must stay within 2× the same render's steady stretches. It uses a new second-difference mode of `clickScore` (`order` 2, normalised by the local RMS of the second difference), because the first difference missed sample-level 10 % gain steps; positive control 20–22 vs a threshold of 6–9. Organ is left out: its top drawbars mask a 10 % step.
  - Envelope test: the per-voice amplitude (new `Graph.voiceAmplitude`, read at block ends) has a second difference under 0.01 of the voice's peak at arrivals, weight ends, births and deaths. Main measures ≤ 6e-4. Glass's strike (0.086) is the positive control, and an injected control-rate step of 10 % at holds fails it (0.09). Level ramps are linear by design (a corner of ~7e-3), so they are left to the sample test.
- **WAVs** (24-bit, 20 s of Kite Day): `/private/tmp/claude-501/pop/kite-day-before.wav` (5d1f216 = 9bd2e44) and `kite-day-nostrike.wav` (glass, organ and reed articulation at 0: the A/B for the strike).
